3.1  General Information 
Treasury Board and Finance supports two computer based methods of filing the Alberta Corporate 
Income Tax Return and Schedules: 
 
1. Paper Return and Schedule Information (AT1 RSI) - A keying summary format of the AT1 
and schedules is the only computer generated paper format allowed by Treasury Board 
and Finance. This option is applicable to taxation years ending in 1998 and subsequent. 
For data capture by Treasury Board and Finance, the AT1 RSI will be scanned using 
Optical Character Recognition technology in place of the conventional manual data entry.  
2. AT1 Net File Return – An electronic format of the AT1 and schedules is also acceptable 
when the return is submitted directly to Treasury Board and Finance via the internet in a 
secure manner using certified software. This option is applicable to taxation years ending 
in 2008 and subsequent. For taxation years beginning January 1, 2025, and later, 
electronic format of the AT1 is required for all corporations except for insurance 
corporations, non-resident corporations, corporations reporting in functional 
currency, and corporations that are exempt from taxation under section 35 of the 
ACTA. 
 
In this chapter, we provide specifications, data requirements and associated business rules for 
developing software for the AT1 RSI  and the AT1 Net File Returns. The data requirements and 
associated business rules are mainly the same for the AT1 RSI return and the AT1 Net File Return, 
with some exceptions which are necessitated by the distinctly different nature of the paper and 
electronic filing methods.  
 
Treasury Board and Finance  does not  accept software that produces facsimile or computer -
generated representations of the AT1 tax return and schedules in the old format for taxation years 
ending in 1998 and subsequent.  All computer generated returns filed for taxation years ending in 
1998 must be filed in AT1 RSI format using only certified software. If you produce facsimile forms 
for your clients, please provide clear instructions that these facsimile forms are for clien t’s records 
only and are no longer acceptable for filing with Treasury Board an d Finance  for 1998 and 
subsequent taxation years. Commencing May 1  2011, Alberta corporations will have the 
option to file their AT1 returns  in net file format  for taxation years ending in 2008 and 
subsequent.  
 
All software developers must comply with the rules and requirements as specified in this Software 
Certification Manual.  In particular for Alberta, the business rules provided to calculate/determine 
the value of each field on every schedule must be adhered  to exactly as specified in the cross -
reference tables in section 3.2.3.  Treasury Board and Finance  reserves the right to reject any 
software generated paper and electronic versions of the AT1 return from any tax preparer who 
deviates from the requirements and specifications of this Manual. 
  
  
Filing Requirements for 1999 and Subsequent Taxation Years 
Effective for 1999 taxation years and subsequent, there is no longer a requirement to file the T2 
RSI or T2, T2 schedules and financial statements or GIFI (General Index of Financial Information) 
with the AT1 package. In other words, software certified for  1999 and subsequent taxation years 
must specify that only the AT1 tax return and schedules are filed with Treasury Board and Finance 
for taxation years ending in 1999 and subsequent.  
 
 
Exempt From Filing: 
If it is determined that the corporation is exempt from filing (see the business rules for completion 
of form 000), you are not required to send any notification to Treasury Board and Finance.  In other 
words, if the corporation is exempt from filing, do not mail the AT1 RSI or transmit the AT1 Net File 

===== PAGE 4 =====
Page 3-4 
Version 2025.2 
August 2025 
   
Classification: Protected A Classification: Protected A 
Return.  Please ensure that your clients are aware that any documents generated for their records 
are to be retained on their file and not submitted to Treasury Board and Finance. 
 
 
Certification of the AT1 Return: 
Filing the AT1 RSI or AT1 Net File Return is equivalent to filing the department printed paper AT1 
and schedules. However, unlike CRA, Treasury Board and Finance  does not require a signature 
on the AT1 RSI. There is no certification form to be completed and maintained by the client.   
 
Amended Returns: 
Amended returns can be filed with Treasury Board and Finance in AT1 RSI format or by net file.   
 
Questions Regarding Chapter 3: 
If you have any questions regarding the specifications in this chapter, please contact us by email 
at TBF.citsoftwarecertification@gov.ab.ca.  
3.1.1 Important Note to Software Developers 
 
Changes for Version 2025.2: 
 
Since release 2025.1 
  
• Net file error message 20165 has been added to Section 3.3.14. 
• Lines 101, 103, & 105 have been added to the AT1.  
 
3.1.2 Software Certification Process for Alberta 
You must develop the logic for your AT1 package incorporating all the business rule edits 
specified in the cross-reference tables (section 3.2.3) and format the AT1 RSI output according to 
the formatting specifications (section 3.2.1).  To file the AT1 return electronically, the 
requirements for AT1 Net File Returns must be met (see section 3.3). You must also conduct 
internal testing on your calculation edits to ensure you have met the business rule edit 
requirements prior to commencing software certification.  Alberta’s test cases will be used to 
verify the software’s calculations and AT1 return formatting.  
 
Software developers will request approval of AT1 Net File format separately from AT1 RSI format.     
Separate approval letters will be issued for each type of software developed.  If the software 
developer submits requests for approval of both filing formats, they may expect to receive two 
different software certification letters. 
 
Once Treasury Board and Finance have reviewed and verified your test case output, we will issue 
a letter to confirm that the Alberta portion of your tax preparation software is approved. Please note 
that if an AT1 return is submitted without a valid approval code or with schedules that were not 
certified or in a format that is not certified, then the return will be rejected and the tax preparer will 
be required to re -file the return either using certified software that contains a valid approval code 
or usin g the department printed forms.  It is critical that a valid approval code appear on your 
production AT1 RSIs or AT1 Net File Returns. 
3.2 AT1 RSI Return Format 
This section includes the AT1 RSI requirements, the cross -reference tables that specify the data 
description, composition and business rules for the AT1 return and schedules.  
For the purpose of the AT1 RSI, the AT1 and schedules have been assigned a three digit form 
identifier number (for example the AT1 will be form 000, Schedule 1 will be form 001, etc…).  
Departmental printed forms will maintain the current schedule naming convention with the addition 

===== PAGE 5 =====
Page 3-5 
Version 2025.2 
August 2025 
   
Classification: Protected A Classification: Protected A 
of new schedules for the 1998 and Subsequent AT1 package.  Fields on the return and schedules 
have been renumbered using a three digit field code. 
3.2.1 AT1 Return and Schedule Information (AT1 RSI) Requirements 
The AT1 RSI is the only paper output from certified software that Treasury Board and Finance  will 
accept; its use is mandatory for the paper component.  The computer generated and laser facsimile 
AT1 and Schedules will no longer be acceptable to Treasury Board and Finance if filed after January 
1999 for taxation years ending in 1998 and subsequent. If your clients require a computer generated 
or laser facsimile version of the departmental forms for their records, clear directions must be 
provided to instruct clients not to file these forms with Treasury Board and Finance. 
 
Along with the implementation of the AT1 RSI, Treasury Board and Finance  is also implementing 
OCR technology for the purpose of data capture rather than manual data entry as was done in the 
past. As a result, the AT1 RSI specifications were developed for this purpose and are different from 
the T2 RSI specifications as the federal government utilizes a manual data entry process.  
 
Approval of the AT1 RSI is based on adherence to the following formatting specifications and to the 
specifications in the cross-reference tables (see section 3.2.3).  The cross -reference tables list the 
data capture fields, the business rules for each of these fields and specify whether fields are 
mandatory, conditional or optional for the purpos e of printing the field on the AT1 RSI.  The layout 
of the AT1 RSI must conform to the requirements that follow and the edits used to derive the field 
values must be determined according to the requirements spelled out in the cross-reference tables. 
3.2.1.1 Definitions 
 The AT1 package consists of the AT1, plus all its supporting Schedules.  
 Schedule ID refers to the 3 digit numeric string specific to each of the forms.  A three -digit 
Schedule number on the AT1 RSI identifies all forms in the AT1 package.  This is referred to 
as the Schedule ID (i.e., AT1 is referred to as 000, AT1 Schedule 1 as 001 and so on). 
 Field ID refers to the 3 digit numeric string denoting the number of a specific Field on a 
Schedule.  For instance, Field ID 034 on the AT1 refers to the field of the Corporate Account 
Number (CAN). 
 Occurrence ID refers to the 3 digit numeric string denoting the occurrence of a particular Field 
for a given Schedule.  Occurrence always defaults to 001, except where multiple occurrences 
pertain.  Multiple occurrences appear in tables in some of the supporting Schedules (e.g., AT1 
Schedule 1, Field 041). 
 Line Item ID refers to the 11 character string beginning with two delimiter characters ##, 
followed by a 9 digit string containing Schedule ID, Field ID and Occurrence ID.  
 Value of Line Item ID refers to the content associated with a particular Line Item ID.  The 
content for a particular Value of Line Item ID is determined by the Business Rules in the Cross-
reference Tables.  The Value of Line Item ID can be either alpha, al phanumeric or strictly 
numeric. 
 Line Item refers to the combination of the Line Item ID and the Value of Line Item ID.  In other 
words, it’s comprised of the delimiter character, the Schedule ID, the Field ID, the Occurrence 
ID and the Value of Line Item ID in the format prescribed. 
 In the example shown below, ##000041001 is the Line Item ID and Name of Corporation is the 
Value of Line Item ID.  Collectively, these are referred to as a Line Item. 
 
##000041001     Name of Corporation 
3.2.1.2 General Specifications 
 Output to the AT1 RSI must use the convention shown in the sample AT1 RSI for all forms in 
the AT1 package. 
 The ASCII code equivalents for the allowable characters on the AT1 RSI are specified in 
section 3.2.1.18. 

===== PAGE 6 =====
Page 3-6 
Version 2025.2 
August 2025 
   
Classification: Protected A Classification: Protected A 
 When a form is printed, all mandatory Field IDs must be output to the AT1 RSI. In addition, all 
optional and conditional fields with values other than zero or null must also be printed. Optional 
and conditional fields with values equal to zero or null must  not appear on the AT1 RSI. See 
the definition of mandatory, optional and conditional in section 3.2.3. and refer to the column 
headed “M O X” in the Cross -reference tables to determine if a field is mandatory, optional or 
conditional. 
 It should not be possible to print the AT1 RSI if mandatory Field IDs are not completed. If the 
value of a mandatory field cannot be determined the value must default to zero.  
 Schedules that are not applicable must not be printed on the AT1 RSI (i.e. the criteria for 
completing a schedule cannot be met or the value of all Line Items is zero or null).  
 In no instance can two number signs appear together (##), with the exception of where they 
appear as the delimiter character indicating a new Line Item.  A single number sign (#) can 
appear in the Value of Line Item ID (for instance in a customer’s address - Suite #1920). 
 The AT1 RSI, comprised of an AT1 and potentially a number of Schedules, must have page 
breaks between the Schedules.  In other words, where a new Schedule begins, there must be 
a new page in the AT1 RSI. 
 Some Schedules in the AT1 package have tables, or what could be described as Line Item IDs 
with multiple occurrences.  (see AT1 Schedule 1, page 2).  A top/down approach should be 
used for these situations.  The example below shows how multiple occurrences should appear 
on the AT1 RSI output (note that it is not mandatory to sort in numerical order, but it is 
preferred): 
 
##001041001     Name  of  Corporation 
##001041002     Name  of  Corporation 
##001041003     Name  of  Corporation 
##001043001     123456789 
##001043002     987654321 
##001043003     564738930 
##001045001     987654 
##001045002     987654 
##001045003     987654 
3.2.1.3 Paper 
 AT1 RSI must be output to 8.5” x 11”  (216mm x 279mm) white bond paper.  
 Photocopy paper - 20 lb. Bond is recommended. 
 No colored paper. 
 No heavy gauge paper stocks. 
 No A4 nor legal sized paper, or other non-standard size papers. 
3.2.1.4 Orientation 
 Portrait only. 
3.2.1.5 Layout 
 Each page of the AT1 RSI is comprised of two main parts.  The first part is the Header 
containing four lines only.  The second part is the AT1 RSI Body.  The Body consists of two 
columns. 

===== PAGE 7 =====
Page 3-7 
Version 2025.2 
August 2025 
   
Classification: Protected A Classification: Protected A 
 The first column of the Body must contain the Line Item ID.  There must be no spacing between 
these 11 characters. 
 The second column of the Body must contain the Value of Line Item ID.  The allowable length 
of the Value of Line Item ID is defined in the Cross-reference Tables. 
 For a Line Item to be considered complete and therefore allowed to be output to the AT1 RSI, 
it must contain both a Line Item ID and a Value of Line Item ID. 
 For each Line Item, column 1 is reserved for the Line Item ID (except where the Value of Line 
Item ID wraps to a second/subsequent line).  This is shown in the example below:  
 
##000011001     Field  Atkinson  Perraton  Meyers  
Norris  Penny  Winspear  McNicol  Barristers  and  
Solicitors  
 For each Line Item, Column 2 is reserved for the Value of Line Item ID corresponding to a 
specific Line Item ID. 
 Five spaces must be provided between column 1 and column 2.  Put another way, 5 spaces 
must appear between the Line Item ID and the Value of Line Item ID. 
 Line items should appear in numeric order sequence within the given Schedule.  
 Where the Value of Line Item ID is alpha or alphanumeric, there must be two spaces between 
all distinct words.  Where the Value of Line Item ID is numeric, no spaces should appear 
between the digits (i.e., 1000).  When referring to the Software Certificati on Manual in regard 
to field length, do not add the additional interword spaces to the field length that is defined in 
the Cross-reference Tables.  This additional spacing requirement is strictly for the RSI output.  
An example showing double space between words follows here: 
 
##000018001     Accounting  Company  123  Ltd. 
 
 Each new delimiter character (##) represents the start of a new Line Item ID as shown in the 
example below: 
 
##000011001     Field  Atkinson  Perraton  Meyers  
Norris  Penny  Winspear  McNicol  Barristers  and  
Solicitors 
##000012001     Suite  1090,  Edmonton  Place 
##000013001     456789  Street  &  Avenue 
##000014001     Edmonton 
##000015001     AB 
 
 For alpha or alphanumeric strings in Value of Line Item ID wrapping to a second line (as shown 
in the first line in the example above), the text on the second (and subsequent) line(s) should 
begin at the left margin.  Words should not be broken between lines; do not use hyphens at the 
end of a line.  Hyphenated words are allowed as long as they appear completely on one line.  
3.2.1.6 Output 
 Black output only (no color inks/toners). 
 Laser or inkjet output recommended. 
 Minimum requirement is 300 dpi (dots per inch) resolution. 
 Simplex (one-sided) printing only. 

===== PAGE 8 =====
Page 3-8 
Version 2025.2 
August 2025 
   
Classification: Protected A Classification: Protected A 
 Print only those fields with values other than 0 (zero) unless the field is identified as 
‘mandatory’ in the Cross-reference Tables; do not print ‘blank’ fields on the AT1 RSI. 
3.2.1.7 Margins 
 These margins are to be used on all pages of the AT1 RSI. 
 Top = 2.0” (including 0.75” blank space at the top of the page plus Header)  
 Bottom = 0.75” 
 Left = 0.75”  
 Right = 0.75” 
3.2.1.8 Alignment 
 All fields must be left justified only; full justification is not allowed. 
3.2.1.9 Tabs 
 No tab stops are to be used. 
3.2.1.10  Font 
 The recommended font of choice is Courier; the second recommended font of choice is Arial.  
In both of these cases, normal character spacing should be used, no subscript nor 
superscript characters.  Twelve (12) or eleven (11) point fonts are acceptable with 12 point 
being the recommended size. 
 Its formatting should be regular (no bolding, italics etc.), 12 or 11 point, normal character 
spacing (no condensed or expanded text, kerning is normal), no subscript nor superscript 
characters. 
3.2.1.11  Leading/Kerning 
 Leading (line spacing) between lines on the AT1 RSI must be 1.5 lines.  
 Spacing between characters (kerning) should be normal. 
3.2.1.12  Header 
 The Header is comprised of three lines as follows: CAN (Corporate Account Number), TYE 
(Tax Year End), and PAGE. 
 Spacing between these lines should be 1.5 lines. 
 The first line must contain the prefix ##, followed by the title AT1 RETURN AND SCHEDULE 
INFORMATION (in uppercase letters with 2 spaces between words). 
 
##AT1  RETURN  AND  SCHEDULE  INFORMATION 
##CAN     1234567890  
##TYE     yyyymmdd 
##PAGE     8  of  8  TIM WOO INC. 
 
 The second line must contain the prefix ##, followed by the abbreviation CAN (in uppercase 
letters, no spacing between characters), followed by 5 spaces (as shown below), followed by 
the 9 or 10  digit string indicating the Corporate Account Number for the taxpayer (e.g., 
1234567890). 
 The third line must contain the prefix ##, followed by the abbreviation TYE (in uppercase letters, 
no spacing between characters), followed by 5 spaces (as shown above), followed by the 8 
digit string indicating Tax Year End for the corporation.  This must  appear in the format 

===== PAGE 9 =====
Page 3-9 
Version 2025.2 
August 2025 
   
Classification: Protected A Classification: Protected A 
yyyymmdd (e.g., 20001031).  Do not output the date in any other format such as 10/31/00, 
31/10/00, Oct 31 2000, 31-Oct-00, etc… 
 The fourth line must contain the prefix ##, followed by the word PAGE (in uppercase letters, no 
spacing between characters), followed by 5 spaces (as shown above), followed by the page 
number out of the total number of pages (e.g., 6  of  6) , followed by the legal name of the 
corporation.  There should be two (2) spaces between the ‘6’, the ‘of’, the ‘6’ and the legal name 
of the corporation.  
 Header information must be printed on each page of the AT1 RSI. 
3.2.1.13  Footer 
 Footers must not be included on the AT1 RSI. 
3.2.1.14  Formatting 
 No shading, nor borders on AT1 RSI. 
 No brackets, nor minus signs are to be used to indicate negative values; use ** to indicate a 
negative value. 
 No bolding. 
 No lines, nor slashes on the AT1 RSI. 
3.2.1.15  Specifications for Alpha and Alphanumeric ‘Value of Line Items IDs’ 
 Refer to the listing of the allowable ASCII codes for the set of allowable characters on the 
AT1 RSI. 
 Alpha and alphanumeric ‘Value of Line Item IDs’ can contain the entire set of allowable 
characters on the AT1 RSI. 
 Characters can be in upper and lower case. 
3.2.1.16  Specifications for Numeric ‘Value of Line Item IDs’ 
 Numeric ‘Value of Line Item IDs’ can only contain numbers (with two exceptions described 
below). 
 ** is one of the special character configurations allowed in numeric Value of Line Item IDs.  
 Negative values must be preceded by ** and immediately followed by the value.  There 
should be no spaces between these characters (e.g., **7500).  This example indicates 
negative seven thousand five hundred. 
 Decimals are not to be used in numeric fields except for the percentage fields described later 
in this chapter. 
 Value of Line Item IDs where percentages apply are to use the number of significant digits 
specified in the Cross-reference Tables. 
 
3.2.1.17  Value of Line Item ID Output 
 Provision for alpha, alphanumeric and numeric fields in the AT1 RSI. 
 Dollar values must be printed with NO decimal places.  Do not include other characters like , : 
or ;.  Do not use dollar signs; do not use cents.. (e.g., $100,000.45 must be printed on the 
AT1 RSI as 100000.  Do not print $100000.45, nor $100,000, nor 100,000, nor 100:000, nor 
100;000). 
 Negative values must be represented with the prefix ** (e.g., **100). 
 A Line Item must not be printed over two pages.  A Line Item must appear in its entirety on 
one page.  This can potentially occur in the example shown here where the Value of Line 
Item ID appears on multiple lines of the AT1 RSI: 

===== PAGE 10 =====
Page 3-10 
Version 2025.2 
August 2025 
   
Classification: Protected A Classification: Protected A 
 
##000011001     Alberta  Corporation:  A  Division  of  
the Canadian  Group  of  Companies  providing  service  
to taxpayers  for  over  50 years.

3.2.1.19  Sample AT1 RSI 
The AT1 RSI sample appearing on the following pages only resembles the actual AT1 RSI, and 
does not account for every possible configuration. 
 
##AT1  RETURN  AND  SCHEDULE  INFORMATION 
##CAN     1234567890 
##TYE     19991231 
##PAGE     1  of  4  123456  Alberta  Corporation 
 
##000005001     AB99 
##000010001     123456  Alberta  Corporation 
##000011001     Alberta  Corporation:  A  Division  of  the  
Canadian  Group  of  Companies  providing  service  to  
taxpayers  for  over  50  years 
##000012001     Suite  1090  Edmonton  Place 
##000013001     14519  108  Street 
##000014001     Edmonton

Classification: Protected A Classification: Protected A 
3.2.2 Reference Copies of the Departmental Printed AT1 and Schedules 
The AT1 and schedules are available in PDF format from Treasury Board and Finance website: 
www.finance.alberta.ca/publications/tax_rebates/corporate/forms 
3.2.3 Cross-Reference Tables 
This section provides the schedule name, form identifier number, field numbers, field descriptions 
and formatting, business rules and mapping details for the AT1 and each schedule.  The data in 
these tables applies to both EFILE and the AT1 RSI. The following information will assis t in 
understanding and reading the tables. 
Mandatory/Optional/Conditional (M/O/X) Column: 
Normally, fields with "null" values are not to be included on the AT1 RSI ("0" is a null value for a 
numeric field and "blank" is a null value for an alpha or alphanumeric field).  However, if a line 
item is classified as Mandatory (M), then the line item MUST be provided and “0” must be printed 
if the line item has a null value for a numeric field. Mandatory date fields must be printed and can 
only contain a valid date format; “0” is not an acceptable value for a date field.  Within the 
mandatory classification, there are certain fields that are “Critical”. These “critical mandatory” 
fields MUST be present on the AT1 RSI. Software must disallow the printing/generation of 
the AT1 RSI if any one of these fields has not been specified on the preparer’s AT1.   
These “Critical Mandatory” fields are: 
Field 000010 - Legal Name  
Field 000012 and 000014 – selected Mailing Address fields 
Field 000036 – Taxation Year Beginning 
Field 000037 – Taxation Year Ending 
   
When a line item is classified as Optional (O), then it is up to the tax preparer to either provide the 
line item value or not.  However, an optional line item with a "null" value is not to be included. 
 
When a line item is classified as Conditional (X), if the condition(s) specified in the Business Rules 
column are met, then the line item becomes Mandatory and the rule for Mandatory line items 
applies (i.e. Must exist even for null values).  If the condit ion(s) are not met, then the line item 
becomes Optional and the rule for Optional line items applies (i.e. exists or not is optional but if it 
exists, then the line item value cannot assume a null value). The same holds true for the schedules; 
all schedules (including the AT1) are conditional and must only be printed if the condition at the 
schedule level is met and provided that not all line items have a null value. Within a schedule, there 
may be sections that are conditional, if the condition is met for completing the section, then the 
rules for the specific fields within the section must be followed. For example, if the condition is met 
requiring completion of the Agreement Among Associated Corporations for schedule 001, then 
fields 001041 and 001045 are mandatory and field 001043 is conditional. Otherwise, if the condition 
to complete this section does not apply, then the rules for these fields are to be disregarded.   
Business Rules and Comments Column: 
Generally in the Business Rules column, a field is referenced using the combination of the three -
digit form identifier followed by the three digit field code.  For example, when referring to the 
corporation name on the AT1, the reference is 000010.  Where a field has multiple occurrences, 
the reference will also contain the 3 digit occurrence number.  For example, when referring to the 
third entry of field 043 on schedule 1, the reference is 001043003. 
The business rules also make reference to federal fields.  To distinguish these from Alberta fields, 
the prefix “fed” has been added to clarify when the reference is to a federal field or form.  Note that 
fields from federal forms 100, 101, 125 and 140 hav e 4 digit field numbers rather than the usual 3 
digits.  For example, on federal form 100, accounts receivable field 1060 is referenced as “fed 
1001060”.  See the Appendices in Chapter 1 for valid province/territory, state and country codes.

 Schedule 9 - Alberta 
Scientific Research & 
Experimental Development 
Tax Credit 
    For the purposes of 
Alberta’s SR&ED Tax 
Credit, “eligible 
expenditures” are the 
amounts included in 
federal expenditures of the 
corporation that are in 
respect of SR&ED and are 
carried out in Alberta 
before January 1, 2020. The 
SR&ED Tax Credit may not 
be claimed in respect of 
any such expenditures 
carried out in Alberta after 
that date. 
    
 Alberta Research & 
Experimental Development 
Supplemental Information 
    If form 009 is completed 
then form “Listing of SR & 
ED Projects Claimed in 
Alberta” must be attached 
with the AT1 RSI format. 
The “Listing of SR & ED 
Projects Claimed in 
Alberta” is not required if 
the return is net filed. If the 
taxation year end is on or 
before March 31, 2012, the 
Schedule 9 Supplemental 
must be filed with the 
Schedule 9. The Schedule 
9 Supplemental is not 
required if the return is net 
filed.

012 Gain on Sale of Eligible 
Capital Property for Alberta 
purposes 
$ 1/13 + X If form 014 exists, then 
calculate: 
a = [(014004 + 014008) X 
¾]-014011; if negative, 
value = 0 
A =  014002 + a + 014006 
B = (014014 + 014016 + 
014018) X ¾ 
If B > A, and the tax year 
ends before February 28, 
2000, then value = (B - A) - 
½(014032 - 014034). If 
negative, enter zero. 
 
If B > A, and the tax year 
ends after February 27, 
2000, but before October 18, 
2000, then: 
C = 014042 + 014044 + 
014032 – 014034, if 
negative, enter zero. 
D = (B – A) – (C – 014047 - 
014048) 
E = (014032 – 014034) X ½ 
Value = (D – E) X 8/9. If 
negative, enter zero. 
 
If B > A, and the tax year 
ends after October 17, 2000, 
then: 
C = 014042 + 014044 + 
014032 – 014034, if 
negative, enter zero. 
D = (B – A) – (C – 014047 -
014048) 
E = (014032 – 014034) X ½ 
Value = (D – E) X 2/3. If 
negative, enter zero. 
 
If A is greater than or equal 
to B, enter zero.

039 Cost of acquisitions from 
column 3 that are designated 
immediate expensing 
property (DIEP) 
$ 1/13 + X A DIEP reported in column 4 
is a property acquired after 
April 18, 2021 by a 
corporation that was a 
Canadian-controlled private 
corporation (CCPC) 
throughout the year, which 
became available for use in 
the tax year (before 2024)

12 months after the filing-due

the designation relates. It 
includes all capital property 
subject to the CCA rules 
other than property included 
in Classes 1 to 6, 14.1, 17, 
47, 49 and 51. A property 
can only qualify as DIEP in 
the year in which it becomes 
available for use. See 
subsection 1104(3.1) of the 
federal regulations for more 
information.

3.3  AT1 Net File Return Format 
  
This section is applicable to software developers who wish to integrate in their software the ability to transmit an Alberta 
Corporate Income Tax Net File Return to Treasury Board and Finance.  
 
3.3.1 Introduction 
Treasury Board and Finance, Tax and Revenue Administration Division, has a published web service that will allow 
certified vendors to develop a client interface to electronically submit Alberta Corporate Income Tax Returns.  This 
section of the document will outline the technology and expected format of the return submission, business rules, error 
codes and conditions, software certification process, and present some sample screen interfaces.  
 
Except for the restrictions listed below, all Corporations will be eligible to net file their Alberta AT1 returns.   
 
Restrictions to Alberta Net File include: 
• Taxation years ending prior to 2008 
• Taxation years ending after 2007 but prior to October 1, 2013, for returns containing Schedules 12 to 21 
• Amended returns 
 
3.3.2 Glossary and Acronyms Used 
Readers of this section should have a basic understanding of the terminology used; however, just so that the language 
being referenced is unambiguous, the following words and acronyms are used in this document.  
 
Acronym Definition 
TBF Treasury Board and Finance  
CAN Corporate Account Number 
CIT Corporate Income Tax 
CRA Canada Revenue Agency 
EDI Electronic Data Interchange 
GOA Government Of Alberta 
HTTP Hypertext Transfer Protocol 
HTTPS Hypertext Transfer Protocol Secure 
SCC Software Certification Code 
SOAP Simple Object Access Protocol 
TRA Tax and Revenue Administration 
TYE Tax Year End 
WSDL Web Services Description Language 
XML Extensible Markup Language 
XSD XML Schema Definition 
 
3.3.3 Web Services Overview 
The submission of an Alberta Net File Return will be performed through a TBF-published web service.  The vendor 
software will conform to the published WSDL for method use, and the payload of the SOAP envelopes must conform to 
the published XSD as well as various business rules as identified in the section below.  
 
Failure to adhere to the published software specifications may result in inability to transmit data to AF, errors passing the 
structure or business rule validations, and ultimately the inability to file an Alberta Net File Return.  Error messages 
returned from the web service are designed to be thorough and informative to the software vendor to ease integration 
and provide as much feedback as possible to the end client. 

===== PAGE 230 =====
CHAPTER 3 - Treasury Board and Finance  
 
Page 3-230 
Version 2025.2 
August 2025 
 
It is highly recommended the software always validate the generated XML payload against the XSD before submitting to 
the AF web services; this will reduce failed returns and unnecessary consumption of the web services . 
 
3.3.4 Test Environment Overview 
A test environment will be made available to the vendor for development purposes as well as software certification.  The 
only difference between the test environment and production will be the address of the web service.   
 
The URL to access Web Service in SOFTWARE CERTIFICATION environment is  
https:// citsoftwarecert.finance.gov.ab.ca/CITNetFile-PublicWebServices-context-
root/CITReturnFilingSoap12HttpPort?WSDL 
 
The test environment will be supported during government working hours [8:15 to 16:30 Mountain Standard Time (MST), 
Monday to Friday]. 
 
3.3.4.1 Software Certification Process and Software Certification Code 
As part of the bi-annual software certification process, the software vendor will be requested to contact TRA 
(TBF.citsoftwarecertification@gov.ab.ca) when the software is ready for certification testing.  A test-environment software 
certification code will be provided for the duration of the development testing and software certification process.  
 
After successful certification, the vendor will be provided with a production software certification code and URL that will 
become part of their return submissions. 
 
3.3.5 Service Availability 
The current service availability for the production environment is from 07:00 to 24:00 Mountain Standard Time (MST).  
Daily scheduled down time for system maintenance is between 24:01 to 06:59 MST and on Sunday from 07:00 to 17:00 
MST.  The service availability may increase in the future.   
The ECOM Help Desk is operated by TRA and it provides technical support on web based/electronic applications for all 
TRA external clients.  The Help Desk can be reached by calling 780-427-9424 or sending email to 
ecomhelpdesk@gov.ab.ca. The current hours of service are from 08:15 to 12:00 and 13:00 to 16:30 Mountain Standard 
Time, Monday to Friday, except for government holidays.  For after-hours support, users also have the option of leaving a 
voicemail.  
3.3.6 EDI Schedule Listing  
A new schedule has been introduced for Alberta Net File called the EDI schedule.  This schedule contains many 
important and mandatory elements, including the software certification code, software details, and filer details.

3.3.7 Return XML Format 
Below is a sample XML file that represents the payload of the SOAP envelope.  The file is structured mainly around a 
series of repeating schedules that contain N numbers of line items.  Note that the comments in the file are for illustrative 
purposes only and are not required. 
 
The mandatory order of the XML file is Header, Schedule in ascending schedule number order, EDI schedule and Footer. 
 
The first sample illustrates a file with all mandatory and optional elements provided in the EDI schedule.  The second 
example shows a return with the optional line items left out of the XML.  Both files are valid.  The software vendor may 
determine if they wish to provide empty tag sets ( <tag></tag>  or  <tag/> ) for empty optional elements or just omit the 
line altogether. 
 
Sample 1: All Mandatory and Optional Elements 
 
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="ISO-8859-1"?> 
<ReturnSubmission xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" 
xsi:noNamespaceSchemaLocation="AlbertaCorporateIncomeTaxReturn.xsd"> 
 <Return> 
        <ProgramCode>01</ProgramCode> 
   <Schedule Number="000"> 
             <Value LineItemID="000005001">AB1234</Value>        <!-- SCC --> 
             <Value LineItemID="000010001">Test Case 1 Legal Name</Value>   <!-- Legal Name of Corporation --> 
<Value LineItemID="000012001">9811 109 St</Value> 
<Value LineItemID="000014001">Edmonton</Value> 
<Value LineItemID="000015001">AB</Value> 
<Value LineItemID="000017001">T5K 2L5</Value> 
<Value LineItemID="000025001">Contact XYZ to discuss return</Value> 
<Value LineItemID="000026001">7801234567</Value> 
<Value LineItemID="000028001">2961</Value> 
<Value LineItemID="000029001">1</Value> 
<Value LineItemID="000031001">2</Value> 
<Value LineItemID="000034001">123456789</Value>      <!-- CAN --> 
<Value LineItemID="000035001">123456789RC0001</Value> 
   <Value LineItemID="000036001">20090101</Value>       <!-- Tax Year Begin --> 
   <Value LineItemID="000037001">20091231</Value>       <!-- Tax Year End --> 
   <Value LineItemID="000038001">2</Value>

   <Value LineItemID="EDI053001">Any Street</Value>     <!--ContactAddress2 --> 
   <Value LineItemID="EDI055001">Any City</Value>       <!--ContactCity --> 
   <Value LineItemID="EDI057001">AB</Value>        <!--ContactProvinceState --> 
   <Value LineItemID="EDI059001">T5T 5T5</Value>       <!--ContactPostalZip --> 
   <Value LineItemID="EDI061001">CA</Value>        <!--ContactCountry --> 
   </Schedule> 
 </Return> 
</ReturnSubmission> 
 
Sample 2: All Mandatory and no Optional Elements

EDI017001) condition.  See 3.3.6 for details.

    </xs:complexType> 
  </xs:element> 
 
  <xs:simpleType name="ProgramCode"> 
    <xs:restriction base="xs:string"> 
      <xs:length value="2"/> 
      <xs:pattern value="[0-9]{2}"/> 
    </xs:restriction> 
  </xs:simpleType> 
 
  <xs:simpleType name="LineItemID"> 
    <xs:restriction base="xs:string"> 
      <xs:length value="9"/> 
      <xs:pattern value="\w{3}[0-9]{6}"/> 
    </xs:restriction> 
  </xs:simpleType> 
 
 
  <xs:simpleType name="ValueString"> 
    <xs:restriction base="xs:string"> 
      <xs:maxLength value="175"/> 
    </xs:restriction> 
  </xs:simpleType>

3.3.9 Size File Restrictions 
The maximum size of a single SOAP transmission must not exceed 128 kB.   
 
Note: If transmission is larger than file size limit, an HTTP error code 413 (Request Entity Too Large) is sent back to the 
client as soon as file size limit is reached and connection is broken. If the client is in listening mode, it will get the 
message.  
 
3.3.10 Transmission Time Limit 
The maximum time of a single SOAP transmission must not exceed 60 seconds.   
 
3.3.11 Business Rules and Validation Processing 
When a return is submitted, various levels of validation processing are run.  Even though a file may pass a rudimentary 
check, it may fail a business rule.  For example, the file may be well-formed and valid XML according to the XSD, but the 
Software Certification Code may be invalid.  An SCC validation error is returned and the processing is stopped.  After 
correcting the SCC and resubmitting, there may be an error with the CAN / TYE being a resubmission, or perhaps an 
invalid e-mail address. 
 
A submission is not considered complete or valid until a confirmation code is returned.  No record of invalid  submissions 
are kept by TRA. 
 
It is for these reasons and for the end user experience that we strongly suggest that all submissions are as valid and 
correct as possible before submitting.  This includes validating all field lengths and datatypes according to the business 
rules, and validating the generated XML submission file against a local copy of the XSD before attempting a file 
submission. 
 
Generally, here is the order of processing for a submitted file: 
 
1. XML Size / Transmission time (transport errors) 
• Max file size of 128 kB 
• Max transmission length of 60 seconds 
 
2. XML Well-Formedness and Validty 
• File must validate against the XSD, which will check for structure along with some basic datatype checks.  
 
3. Missing Schedules 
• At a minimum, schedules 000 and EDI must be submitted 
 
4. Duplicated Schedules 
• Schedules  may not appear more than once in a single submission 
 
5. Missing Mandatory Line Items 

===== PAGE 237 =====
CHAPTER 3 - Treasury Board and Finance  
 
Page 3-237 
Version 2025.2 
August 2025 
• The followed mandatory line items must be submitted:  Corporate Account Number (AT1 line 034), Taxation 
Year End (AT1 line 037), and all the line items designated as ‘mandatory’ under the EDI Schedule in Section 
3.3.6. 
 
 
6. Missing Web Service Version 
• Web Service Version is a value provided to the software vendor during the Software Certification Process  
 
7. Missing Software Version 
• Major and Minor version of the software used for the submission.  Must be updated by the software vendor 
per software upgrade. 
 
8. Missing Software Serial Number 
• Serial Number or some other unique identifier to help identify the registrant, submitter, or particular software 
used to submit the return.  May be used in conjunction with the software vendor in identifying and controlling 
fraudulent submissions, and / or for internal reporting and usage tracking purposes.  
 
9. Invalid TYE format  
• TYE must be in the YYYYMMDD format. 
 
10. Future-dated TYE 
• The TYE cannot be future-dated 
 
11. Invalid / Expired SCC or Mismatch of SCCs 
• The SCCs in line item numbers  000005001 and EDI001001 must be equal  
• The SCC must have passed the certification process and must be valid for the range of TYE certified.  This 
will be defined to the software vendor as part of the software certification process.  (e.g.: SCC AB1234 
passes the certification process on 30 Apr 2011.  It is only valid for returns with a TYE between 01 Jan 2011 
and 31 Dec 2011.  If this SCC is used on a net file return submitted on 15 Apr 2011, it will not be accepted.  
If this SCC is used for a net file return with TYE of 31 Mar 2012, it will not be accepted.) 
  
12. Invalid / Unsupported Schedules Exist 
• Each SCC is tied to a list of valid schedules that may be submitted.  These schedules will be provided to you 
as part of the software certification process.  (e.g.: SCC AB1234 is valid only for returns submitted with 
schedules 000, 001, 002, 008, and EDI.  If a return with schedule 009 is net filed with this SCC, it will not be 
accepted.) 
 
13. Invalid CAN format 
• CAN must be 9 or 10 digits 
 
14. Invalid CAN 
• CAN must exist in TRA’s database 
 
15. CAN and TYE combination already exists 
• This denotes a duplicate return situation that is not an amendment. If a CAN and TYE have ever been 
previously submitted (via paper or electronic), the submission will be rejected.  This includes a double -
submission or quick-correction situation: once a confirmation number is returned, that return has been stored 
in the TRA system and the CAN / TYE combination cannot ever again be submitted through Alberta Net File.   
 
16. EDI Field Validations 
• If the filer is a third party service provider (Schedule EDI Line 017 = ‘1’), all the line items designated as 
‘conditional’ under the EDI Schedule Listing in Section 3.3.6 must be submitted.    
• See the chart in the EDI Schedule Listing section for details on each field in the EDI schedule.   
  

===== PAGE 238 =====
CHAPTER 3 - Treasury Board and Finance  
 
Page 3-238 
Version 2025.2 
August 2025 
3.3.12 Declaration Text 
The following text must be presented to the filer before filing a return.  It is mandatory that the end user be presented thi s 
text per submission through the vendor software.  If, upon review of the vendor software, it is determined that the 
disclaimer text was not appropriately presented as part of a return submission, AF reserves the right to reject the vendor 
software during the software certification process or revoke the software’s certification code, thereby disabling it from 
submitting subsequent returns. 
 
The text must be presented exactly as below in a manner that cannot by bypassed.  The submitter must agree to the text 
through the use of a checkbox or other acknowledgement method.  Only after agreeing to the declaration text may the 
software proceed with the submission process. 
 
See the sample vendor screens for an implementation example of the declaration text.  
 
 
 
Declaration 
 
This return meets Alberta Corporate Net File eligibility requirements. 
 
I will not attempt to disrupt Alberta Corporate Net File service by uploading files other than an eligible 
Alberta corporate income tax return.  If I do, Tax and Revenue Administration (TRA) may deny me 
access to electronic services. 
 
I am an authorized signing officer of the corporation, or, if I am acting on behalf of the corporation, an 
authorized signing officer of the corporation has instructed me to file this return.   
 
I certify, or, if filing on behalf of a corporation, an authorized signing officer of the corporation has 
certified, that this electronic return, including schedules, is a true, correct and complete return and 
that the method of computing income for this taxation year is consistent with that of the previous 
taxation year except as specifically disclosed on the return.   
 
   I have read and agree to the above.

3.3.16 Sample SOAP filing message 
This is an example of the raw SOAP message that is sent for filing a return.  Note that the payload is denoted by  <! --
RETURN PAYLOAD -->.  The payload will contain the Return XML as outlined in an earlier section. 
 
 
POST https://URL-OF-SERVICE.AB.CA/CITNetFile-PublicWebServices-context-root/CITReturnFilingSoap12HttpPort HTTP/1.1 
Accept-Encoding: gzip,deflate 
Content-Type: application/soap+xml;charset=UTF-8 
User-Agent: Jakarta Commons-HttpClient/3.1 
Host: 111.111.111.111 
Content-Length: 4907 
 
<soap:Envelope xmlns:soap="http://www.w3.org/2003/05/soap-envelope" xmlns:cit="http://cit.tra.fin.goa/"> 
   <soap:Header/> 
   <soap:Body> 
      <cit:fileReturn> 
         
      <arg0> 
<![CDATA[  
 
<!--SAMPLE RETURN PAYLOAD--> 
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="ISO-8859-1"?>  
<ReturnSubmission xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" 
xsi:noNamespaceSchemaLocation="AlbertaCorporateIncomeTaxReturn.xsd">    
<Return>      
<ProgramCode>01</ProgramCode>      
<Schedule Number="000">        
<Value LineItemID="000005001">AB1234</Value> 
<Value LineItemID="000010001">CORPORATION LEGAL NAME</Value> 
... 
... 
</Schedule>    
 ... 
<Schedule Number="EDI">        
<Value LineItemID="EDI001001">AB1234</Value> 
<Value LineItemID="EDI011001">0.0.1</Value> 
... 
... 
</Schedule>    
</Return>  
</ReturnSubmission> 
<!—END OF SAMPLE RETURN PAYLOAD--> 
 
]]> 
  </arg0> 
</cit:fileReturn> 
   </soap:Body> 
</soap:Envelope>

3.3.18 Transmission Errors 
If a submission has been denied due to a functional or technical error, the client must contact the software vendor for 
assistance.  If the error has been determined to be on the AF side, the software vendor may contact AF to discuss the 
error.  The client shall not contact AF directly for resolving filing errors, and as such will be redirected to the software 
vendor for filing assistance.
